- **shader**: A `&lt;shader&gt;` item is a GLSL ES 3.00 fragment shader. For its time range it reads the composite of every track beneath it and draws the frame that replaces it. Tracks above it draw over the result as usual, and several shaders stack in track order. Where nothing is beneath it, the composite is transparent black, so a shader can draw a whole frame on its own. ([MdSadiqMd/dotfiles](https://github.com/MdSadiqMd/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/agents/skills/cardboard/commands/shader.md))

- **shannon-gap**: Compute the link's **Strehl ratio** — its achieved spectral efficiency divided by the theoretical Shannon ceiling for the same SNR and bandwidth. In optics, Strehl is the achieved peak intensity over the diffraction-limited ideal; here it is achieved bits/Hz over the information-theoretic ideal. A perfect modem sits near 1.0; every dB you bleed to impairments drops it. - **sheets-cell**: The detail view for **one cell**: what was entered, what it evaluates to, and — when it is a formula — the current values of every cell it references. `sheets read` is the list view; this is where an agent goes to find out *why* `D2` shows `#VALUE!`, or to confirm what a formula it wrote is actually computing from. - **sheets-dimensions**: Structural edits that **shift data in place**: insert empty rows or columns at a position (everything below or to the right moves), or delete rows or columns (everything after them moves up or left). `sheets append` only ever adds rows at the end and `sheets clear` empties cells without moving anything; these four are the verbs for "make room in the middle" and "take this row out".
